# Qualification history and design decisions

This is a dated evidence log, including the original M0 decisions. Statements
and commands below describe their recorded revisions; they are not current setup
instructions or a second roadmap. Use the [qualification guide](../../qualification/README.md)
for current test ownership and commands, the [Root guide](../demos/root.md) for
the current hands-on workflow, and the [architecture](../design/canic-system-design.md)
for scope and milestone gates. Old local output paths remain evidence references.

## Qualification guide and original provisioning role fixture — 2026-11-21

The original role-name experiment installed two hub roles and a shard using Toko's
application crates and test harness. Its config and metadata endpoints belong with
the experiment. The installed check references the external harness.
[Original configuration](provisioning-roles/canic.toml) · [Hub endpoint](provisioning-roles/hub.rs) · [Shard endpoint](provisioning-roles/shard.rs) · [Installed check](provisioning-roles/roles.rs)

The pre-cleanup guide records the supplier versions used for the Platform, Testkit,
Auth, Storage, Store and Blob adoption runs, the original greeting journey, and
the sequence of Root maintenance and publication rehearsals. Its detailed run
descriptions remain available at the revision where they were recorded.
[Historical baseline](https://github.com/dragginzgame/canic/blob/d6de92cfcbd4e4d72550f3690641d7cef4d025b0/qualification/README.md#qualified-baseline) · [Root and Store evidence](https://github.com/dragginzgame/canic/blob/d6de92cfcbd4e4d72550f3690641d7cef4d025b0/qualification/README.md#checks-and-evidence)

At Storage adoption the fresh counter and Root checks recorded Storage 0.78.3 and
Testkit 0.110.1. The DB 0.24.4 and Blob 0.107.1 suites recorded direct use of
Storage 0.78.7. The populated Blob 0.100.6 predecessor refused upgrade and
preserved its stable bytes, management report and asset.

## Root journal hard cut — 2026-11-21

The storage codec tests reopened CUJC at two slots, four slots and sparse
placement identities, preserving the original child, Store approval, issued stop
and installation evidence. On PocketIC, the shared and dedicated placement journey
recovered existing assignments after fleet growth and Root replacement.
All seven Store-maintenance cases passed, including recovery after callback loss
and refusal of a predecessor upgrade with a Store installation already issued.

The installed `storage_reopen::` test placed the frozen two-slot CUJ9 record
from `d5f2906` in a current named cell. The candidate reported
`unsupported Root journal format`. The installed module hash, full stable
memory and subnet canister count matched their values before the attempt.

## Indexed shared-shard placement — 2026-11-21

On PocketIC, 20,000 distinct callers signed up through one hub under a
two-shard grant of 10,000 live keys each. Each signup in each group resolved
to its group's allocation. The next signup returned Full. After Root reopened,
lookup and repeat signup for keys 1, 10,000, 10,001 and 20,000 returned their
original principals and generations. The unassigned key still returned Full.
The separate predecessor check populated an inline-layout Root, observed a
refused upgrade, and recovered its original assignment on the old executable.
Run `bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh sharding`. The 100,000 hard ceiling is
an admission bound. This installed load case exercised 10,000 per shard.

## User Directory Wire baseline — 2026-11-21

The normal `make check-wire-endpoints` passed for the seven hub methods
and seven shard methods selected by the default User Directory recipes.
In an isolated copy, inserting a unit variant before `ProvisionRefusal::Database`
shifted its discriminant from 1 to 2. Both Wasms rebuilt, and all three inspection
tests passed, including the names and call modes check. The shared baseline
comparison then exited 1. Only the replies of hub `register` and shard
`provision_user` differed. The method IDs and request schemas matched throughout.
The qualification mutation was confined to the isolated copy.

A fleetless workspace generated by `canic app init --source` passed
`canic app check-wire` with its shipped starter baseline. Selecting
`canic/endpoint-metrics` produced the shared metrics query in both the
application Wasm and the candidate. After accepting it in the fixture,
adding a u32 to the greeting reply failed the schema comparison with all
method IDs, names and modes preserved. A query present only in the Wasm
failed the export comparison before baseline review.

## Wasm Store 0.5.2 to 0.6.0 — 2026-11-21

The Root preservation fixture installed the published 0.5.2 Store selected by Canic
v0.245.0, then upgraded to the ordinary 0.6.0 registry artifact. The original Wasm
digest was `82ef8bf110529c9b32ce04b0ef1320f8e1303964bb5f3b3f81a4a79729ca8f6e`
and the replacement was `71b1d4c3f939634b5259325a9ca2f632ccc448d6bd2deecdd1a2fb07b0806547`.
All three preservation cases passed. Root retained its scan position across its
own upgrade, verified the Store's stable bytes across replacement, and reached
Ready through explicit activation. The same suite passed with the ordinary
0.6.0 Store as original and its maintenance candidate as replacement.

## Schema-selected DB roles — 2026-11-21

With IC DB 0.26.2, the installed two-store hub wrote primary key 1 with values
7 and 19 to different entities, then read both through the same `db!()`
after a heap-replacing upgrade. An upgrade to a hub with a different directory
trapped. The original stable bytes and both reads survived. Run
`bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh db-roles` on the prepared simulator.

The User Directory predecessor run used hub and shard Wasms built from Canic
`v0.245.0` (`6ff659c`) and the existing explicit Storage policies.
With `CANIC_PREDECESSOR_DIRECTORY_ROLES` pointing at those binaries,
`bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh user-directory` passed both installed tests.
This covers reopening the populated single-store layout through the new schema
mount. Changing a deployed roster still requires a directory migration.

## IC DB 0.24.0 schema-owner upgrade — 2026-11-20

The User Directory journey installed the previous IC DB 0.23.22 role Wasms,
registered two accounts on distinct shards, and upgraded the populated hub and
one shard to actors using the shared schema crate's typed stores. Both opened
under their original allocation keys with byte-identical stable memory. The
shard refused session admission after volatile Auth state was lost.

To repeat, set `CANIC_PREDECESSOR_DIRECTORY_ROLES` to a directory containing
the original `user_hub.wasm` and `user_shard.wasm` and run
`bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh user-directory`. The predecessor SHA-256 digests
were `9df6e33240e0e363b85863e10a9b6c0ffec58fe9955af4df2b2e71b5f658ecbc`
(hub) and `6b78bc30a4ca90dd0603bb823efca744f45822eca26809ed14ee126e7ab12d3e`
(shard).

## Stable session and issuer checkpoints — 2026-11-19

With IC Auth 0.48.6, the installed Ed25519 fixture used a distinct governed
engine lease and host record for each owner. A deliberately damaged receiver
candidate refused reopen and its predecessor still served the live session.
Two successful upgrades required new Root enrollment; the second preserved
logout and denied the original credential. The issuer reconciled its completed
signature and retained a pending renewal whose old callback ticket could no
longer record a nonce. No external signer or certification tree was involved.
The volatile receiver upgrade-loss case remains covered. Host admits a receiver
upgrade before or after grant approval while fencing the authoritative Root in
both planning orders.
Legacy enrollment and late-outcome research at `fa92d886` recorded below still
applies. IC Auth now supplies checked, single-use checkpoints for these engines.

## Auth Wire 0.45.0 and Blob Storage 0.107.0 — 2026-11-19

Both workspaces resolved IC Wire 0.26.1. The generator changed one comment in
the browser runtime. Its schemas and native frame fixtures remained byte-identical.
Four native Auth tests, 20 installed Auth tests, seven browser adapter tests and
11 installed Chromium cases passed with the existing browser client.

All ten installed Blob tests passed, including catalog rollback, the populated
predecessor refusal and the managed fleet journey. The Blob registry archive
SHA-256 was `7a58cbe9ee13d687812bbdfb9772459e0c963588be4249ec765e349b15bce7c2`.
Supplier and lockfile fingerprints matched before and after the installed run.

## Wasm Store 0.4.6 to 0.5.0 — 2026-11-19

The installed Root preservation test used the ordinary Store 0.4.6 Wasm from
Canic v0.234.0 as its original and the ordinary registry-built 0.5.0 Wasm as
its replacement. Their SHA-256 digests were
`db0d1496198dacb8a0696642cf1f92ccb42eef8a8e9ecaebe1b14aaebacbcdee` and
`6df5cad81f2d334aef17533eb7bb2fae28d3ec876155df77b275afdd6123a936`.
With live, deleted and cancelled releases in the catalog, Root retained the scan

## Shared Toko entities and ordinary provisioning functions — 2026-11-19

For #44, an isolated copy of the Toko working tree (base `e67ddf1f`)
passed the installed `signup` and `user_shard` tests using Canic’s
`provisioning::new_ulid`, first on fresh installs and again with populated
hub/shard predecessors. The predecessor SHA-256 prefixes were `244649dc` and
`07b63ba5`, and the rebuilt candidates were `c24b14f9` and `f85f5974`.
The test workspace selected the local Canic products and Store 0.5.0,
copying the four Auth issuer Wire budget fields across the two client
codec versions. The separate live Internet Identity case was skipped.
Canic’s installed `sharding` (three tests) and `user-directory` (one test)
also passed.

Canic #43 used an isolated copy of the Toko shared-schema working tree (base
`bdc2bed`) with IC DB 0.23.12. The hub and shard each imported one external
entity and checked the fingerprint of the attached database against that
single-entity schema. Six native application tests passed. Both hosts passed
native and Wasm Clippy. The installed role-name change preserved the hub's
stable bytes. The installed signup witness restored a pending hub reservation
after the shard committed, then confirmed the original ID and shard with
identical shard bytes on retry. A later retry preserved the renamed profile.
The shard refused an unauthorized caller. Both signup and shard checks passed
again with populated hub/shard predecessors (SHA-256 prefixes `ea5e29e5` and
`6741e3da`). The separate live II case was skipped.

## IC DB 0.23.11 and portable endpoint handlers — 2026-11-19

For Canic #42 the adapter switched to the complete unique-index lookup and
checked the returned entity's canonical encoding. The Canic workspace locked
IC DB 0.23.12 and passed 17 native tests with provisioning enabled, including
refusal of a non-unique account index. The isolated consumer locked 0.23.11,
built three role artifacts and passed the installed role, signup/retry and
shard checks, including signup and shard upgrades from populated predecessors.

The provisioning lookup examined at most one candidate. Confirmation accepted
a 1,024-byte result and left the reservation pending for a 1,025-byte result.
IC DB reports encoded output size after decoding. Both installed DB witness
cases passed. The isolated Toko fixture below built all three role artifacts,
preserved storage through a role-name change, and passed signup and shard
checks on fresh and predecessor installations. The live II case was skipped.

The starter and greeting actors passed native and Wasm Clippy. The installed
CLI journey called the greeting and upgraded the same canister to v2.

## Conventional provisioning mount — 2026-11-18

The role-name extension used the same hub package for `user_hub` and
`project_hub`, with an imported alias for `ProvisioningRole::Hub`. The ordinary
app builder produced both artifacts from the configured roles. The installed
fixture read both names, upgraded between them, and compared the full stable
image and database key. A child using `ProvisioningRole::Shard` read its
standalone Rust name `account_archive`. Replacing the behavior with `"hub"`
failed with E0308; selecting Shard for the hub layout failed with E0080.

An isolated Toko working tree at base `bdc2bed7857b` used a single mount in
each role. The shared resource validated and exported the display name. The hub
and shard used their existing keys, owners, placement domain and Wire limits.
Seven native account tests, native and Wasm Clippy, and both release Wasm builds
passed. A Wasm build also checked an app-owned synchronous ULID generator with
a custom registration scope.

The installed `signup` and `user_shard` tests passed twice, with fresh actors
and with `TOKO_PREDECESSOR_FLEET_DIR` selecting Toko’s existing binaries.
Predecessor SHA-256 was
`ea5e29e5b47cd655932276d105fff996ee0b94295d93c38fe2a65e4c53a73aad` (hub)
and `6741e3da61e0708b36a844e2e44d5c3188a3133dffe619862be6ec732378168e` (shard).
Restoring the pending hub after shard commit simulated a lost receipt. The retry
confirmed the original route and left the shard bytes intact. The installed
shard rejected invalid replay data. The separate live II browser case stayed
outside this run. No supplier or active Toko source changed.

## Native provisioning declarations — 2026-11-18

An isolated copy of Toko’s working tree at `bdc2bed` used the local Canic
candidate and IC DB 0.23.10. Both role crates removed the guards and imports
from their provisioning declarations. The shared display-name validator used
portable Rust validation and panic on refusal. The hub’s IC ID generator and
each actor’s store accessor remained Wasm-only. Both roles passed native
all-target Clippy with warnings denied and compiled for release Wasm.
The installed signup test retained the original route on retry and the
shard test preserved accounts through upgrade. The live II browser fixture
was outside this run.

## Blob Storage 0.106.0 — 2026-11-18

Both workspaces selected the registry archive with SHA-256
`dcdcba8e04e4fd043a55d60f4ffce34c8eb47166d6604fe3da30fb96ea563347`.
Blob and Canic resolved the same Wire 0.25.0 limits type. The default Canic

The supplier and lockfile fingerprints matched before and after the run.

## Account provisioning on a placed shard — 2026-11-18

An isolated copy of the in-progress Toko tree at base `bdc2bed7857b` bound
its existing hub and shard entities to Canic provisioning. The hub kept
Toko’s ULID generator, account key domain and display-name validation. Both
tables, their stable keys and public method frames stayed intact. Root still
owns the original generation. Legacy `canic-core/src/domain/policy/pure/placement/sharding/hrw.rs`
(last selector change `c0f1dd414673`) and the legacy storage sharding tests
support retaining the existing selector and capacity owner.

The installed `signup` and `user_shard` suites exercised two managed shards,
pool exhaustion, caller isolation, an unrelated parent, retry after rename and
populated upgrade. The application builds used `canic app build --app toko`
with its config. The native test command in the isolated Toko workspace was
`cargo test --locked --offline --manifest-path qualification/Cargo.toml --test signup --test user_shard`.
The new hub also passed signup against the unmodified, registry-built
0.226.0 predecessor shard identified below. The separate browser II case
remained outside this gate.

Two simulator-only variants exercised the message boundaries. Two simultaneous
signup requests saw unusable acknowledgements after the shard committed. Both
returned pending. Retrying with a different name retained the first ID, route
and original payload. An upgrade with that reservation pending preserved the
entire hub stable image and refused the stale user's retry under closed Auth.
In the second variant Root reported a different generation at confirmation
with the same principal. The hub kept the route pending; a subsequent exact
observation permitted confirmation. No fault path is compiled into the product.

The native IC DB regression reopens the populated allocation and completes
the retained reservation, refuses mismatched IDs and receipts, and checks
compatibility with the previous placement key and flat Wire frame. Run
`make db-test db-clippy` and `bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh sharding`.
A successful authenticated user retry after a volatile-receiver upgrade
still depends on the separate Auth recovery contract.

## Shard parent allocation — 2026-11-18

For [Canic #37](https://github.com/dragginzgame/canic/issues/37), an isolated copy
of Toko's in-progress shard at base `bdc2bed7857b` built first against crates.io
Canic 0.226.0 with its explicit `toko.user.shard.hub.v1` allocation, then
against the candidate with that argument removed. Wasm SHA-256 digests:

- Published-library predecessor: `8c9b7bb0d449f87d047a0c900c5c42ecf723610c01de9a15072577602bc5a9ff`.
- Default-key candidate: `057cef84dcbf2e56bcd02e4310afa280e5c3b25f690881db743cd1deded4cac1`.

The isolated `user_shard` installed test ran once with a fresh candidate and
again with `TOKO_PREDECESSOR_FLEET_DIR` selecting the populated predecessor.
Both preserved the entire stable image and original provisioning receipt.
Calls through the approved hub succeeded. An unrelated canister and anonymous
ingress were refused. Corrupting either the original cell header or its principal
on separate simulator copies trapped during upgrade and left the damaged
images untouched.

A second registry-built predecessor used `toko.user.shard.original.v1`.
The matching `parent_storage_key` reopened it. The default-key candidate
refused that image, preserved its bytes and left the original hub route usable.
All eight native shard tests also passed. The Toko checkout was read-only.

## Endpoint attributes and generated role layout — 2026-11-18

Compared the profile entry and worker built by `make profile-test` against
the same fixture, features and build command at release `b2ccf4e1b5ab`.
The entry shrank from 396,523 to 395,075 bytes and the worker from 313,599 to
313,553 bytes. Their export names and kinds and their IC imports matched.
The entry’s Wasm-target normal/build graph replaced the host proc macro `paste`
with `canic-macros`; both graphs used the same Wire and Platform versions.
The installed profile test checked complete response frames, caller guards
and synchronous rollback after a refused reply encoding.

In an isolated Toko checkout the installed `signup` and `user_shard` tests
passed with scoped hub and shard endpoints, an asynchronous signup and a
parent-guarded provisioning contract. Additional installed hub probes called
a bare raw-identifier update with its derived Wire ID and observed an explicit
refusal before decoding a request for an unscoped session. The browser II
case has a separate runner.

A fresh `canic app init` and `canic app build --canister app` produced the
stateless greeting, probe and readiness exports. The same generated project
then built a hub with one schema entity, role setup, a bare update, one init
and one post-upgrade. The Host layout regression checks misplaced declarations
and a per-package override alongside another generated package with defaults.
Moving the generated hub’s endpoints to `src/api` made the CLI build report
the expected directory; setting its own `endpoints = "src/api"` restored the build.

## Declarative Toko database roles — 2026-11-18

An isolated Toko working copy based on `417ee72c5852` replaced both handwritten
lifecycle modules and its shared database declaration with `canic::mount_db_role!`.
The native builds consumed IC DB 0.23.8's generated schema. All 16 native hub
and shard tests passed and neither normal native dependency graph included
IC Platform Runtime. Each Wasm exported exactly one init, one post-upgrade and
one readiness method.

The installed `signup` and `user_shard` tests passed for fresh binaries and
again after populating and upgrading the prior handwritten roles. Both complete
stable images survived upgrade byte-for-byte. The shard preserved its original
parent and provisioning receipt. The hub's controller refresh remained closed
after upgrade. Additional probes refused a zero hub nonce, invalid shard
context, an incorrect installer and failed reopen. Readiness remained an
observation of successful store attachment.

| Role | Handwritten predecessor SHA-256 | Declarative candidate SHA-256 |

## Backup recovery after operator disconnection — 2026-11-18

The installed witness on Canic 0.224.0 submitted restore start, acceptance and
opening once apiece and discarded their ingress handles. Each completion came
from Root's lookup against the saved request. After acceptance the fixture
replaced Root's controller. The previous controller lost lookup and execution
authority, and Root refused the successor's attempt to issue Open under the
previous controller's plan. Restoring the original controller let it observe
acceptance; the counter still refused increments until explicitly opened.

The two routes in `make backup-signer-test` use IC Backup 0.105.6, IC Host Sign
0.1.0 and a disposable Linux keyring. The offline restore check compares the
receipt and reserved signatures, network requests and observations with the
live operation. `bash scripts/dev/test-root.sh snapshot::` separately checks
both callback-loss windows, including retained Pending across a Root upgrade.
This qualification covers one capture and restore of the same counter. The
operator lifecycle calls use Testkit ingress; Backup's snapshot steps use the
real external signer. The counter has no external participants to reconcile.

## Retained Toko shard store

On 2026-11-18 an isolated copy of Toko used Canic’s optional `db-store` with
registry IC DB 0.23.6 and Storage 0.78.2. Its original authority `user_shard`,
prefix `toko.user.shard.`, default directory configuration, two keys, bounded
hub cell and Toko incarnation derivation were retained. This follows the legacy
review of reopened references and exact snapshots at
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801` (storage regressions under
“Versioned Wasm Store decision”). IC DB now owns schema admission.

`canic app build --config apps/toko/canic.toml --app toko --canister user_shard`
produced candidate SHA-256
`6d7af258801986162d91b96d4cc69237337b9b3b02dd066e037b1bced9ec2c03`.
Toko's installed `user_shard` test ran with `TOKO_PREDECESSOR_SHARD_WASM`
selecting `07354bf02d7c814c5dcb4659ca41376eaaef1274a1b872a7db9d16d914ba1c4b`.
It provisioned a user through the hub before upgrade, compared complete stable
memory after upgrade, refused an unauthorized caller and a conflicting binding,
and returned the original receipt on replay. Empty and foreign stable backing
both rejected upgrade and retained their original bytes. The in-repo DB gate
also opens a directly created populated supplier database and checks both
allocation IDs and the adjacent principal bytes. The default Canic Wasm graph
continues to exclude IC DB and Storage.

## Stateful fleet Auth receivers — 2026-11-18

An isolated working copy of Toko at base
`417ee72c58529490b8e3eaf9a7bb13ff67075af1`, with local consumer changes,
built both roles with `canic app build --config apps/toko/canic.toml --app toko`.
Canic used registry IC Auth 0.48.0. The resulting hub Wasm SHA-256 was
`df587b09984bf8505390aa9da17af5a6cf8c2e51697e354aa46ca9e8126584a2`;
the shard was `149f9b2c9ce13e729d7a8ca27ba4ecf8e0865c4860cf9f2bccc2b06f00dbaf21`.

The installed signup and user_shard tests passed. They exercised managed shard
placement, separate receiver admission and upgrade with populated application
state. The headless II fixture recovered an interrupted logout after a browser
reload, checked shard cleanup and read the original profile after fresh sign-in.
A canister upgrade reopens application state with Auth closed. Recovering a
receiver after its volatile replay and logout state is lost requires the
[IC Auth recovery contract](https://github.com/dragginzgame/ic-auth/issues/4)
and its [installed proof](https://github.com/dragginzgame/ic-auth/issues/5).

Canic's Auth, browser, profile and publication suites passed separately. The
publication suite rebuilt a generated starter. The stateful hub and shard in
the installed signup test used Toko's existing application sources.

## IC Wire 0.25.0 — 2026-11-18

Canic and its qualification workspace now depend directly on registry IC Wire
0.25.0. Their lockfiles also select 0.24.6 through IC Auth 0.47.4, IC Auth Wire
0.43.0, Blob 0.105.0 and the Wasm Store protocol 0.4.1. Auth's supplier types
and TypeScript schema registry use the same re-exported Wire crate. Regenerating
the browser sources yielded an empty diff. Canic projects the Store's complete
metadata and chunk budgets from the published constants into its codec.

The installed counter preservation test also ran with the retained 0.24.6
counter as its original executable (SHA-256
`79419fc0e85e89c112e86be122a0eaa7041a03ded0ee0f20edb68e9aa74198f3`)
and the 0.25.0 v2 candidate
(`02d0fd388cb1521535380a17b5fdb5ddbabd60f7886ea86ba401ab804bddb2e7`).
The populated value, installation authority and closed fence survived.
The rejected and incompatible candidates preserved the original state.
Installed validation passed both Backup routes, the Root callback-loss and
upgrade case, 34 Store tests, 20 Auth tests and 10 Blob tests. All seven
browser-client tests and the repository lint gate also passed.

## Concurrent Wasm build outputs — 2026-11-18

Cargo 1.100 reports the same final cdylib path for different feature selections.
Canic now gives each Cargo invocation a private target directory for its final
outputs and shares Cargo's configured build directory for intermediates. The
artifact report and publication use the same bounded read. The installed
`make counter-test` launches the base and incompatible variants together, then
independently inspects both returned paths and compares their hashes with the
build receipts. The installed counter accepts its compatible upgrade and refuses
the incompatible one.

## Fenced recovery of the managed counter — 2026-11-18

The maintainer selected the installing Root to retain generations and mediate
explicit operator acceptance and opening. Reused the legacy checkpoint and

## Independent hub and shard grants under one Root — 2026-11-18

On the local working tree based on `1e1bb590`, the installed Auth suite passed
20 cases. The new single-Root witnesses transferred a shard to Root as sole
controller, leaving the hub under the operator. The former installer could
no longer refresh the shard directly. A distinct caller could neither
configure nor revoke grants nor trigger a protected refresh. Neither receiver
acquired a session from the other's admission. After either grant was revoked
and its receiver refreshed, a fresh login and the existing session both
returned authority-unavailable there. The other receiver still served its
existing session and admitted the fresh login.

The capacity witness filled 32 slots, revoked one, and checked exact retries,
conflict refusal and refusal of a 33rd principal. The CLI witness loaded the
old single-table declaration, retained the same hub operation when a second
table was added, and independently applied and revoked the shard approval.
The existing two-Root Chromium 156.0.8078.4 journey passed all eleven cases.

## Auth admission reply recovery — 2026-11-18

The installed Ed25519 profile journey passed eleven cases in 37.31 seconds

expiry. The first renderer performed zero profile writes. The second wrote

## Backup direct signer and original-request recovery — 2026-11-18

The installed Linux witness used registry Backup 0.105.4, Host Sign 0.1.0,

had SHA-256 `162219b610b0997be248df5f86e2c3184d898781c237f00753531897ead919de`.

journal and reconstructing the signer preserved the original request ID and journal
bytes. Normal advance spent no signature and sent no request. A different signer
manifest was refused. Explicit observation issued one original-request status
lookup and capture completed. Offline receipt verification passed. Restore
replaced the modified 64 KiB of stable memory byte-for-byte and left the child
stopped. This fixture has one direct controller and synthetic application authority.

The follow-up Canic consumer run exercised a partial success frame, output one
byte over Tooling’s frame limit, process death, deadline expiry and caller
cancellation after process launch. Dropping the connection reaped each provider.
Cold journal reopen retained one spent signing reservation, no original request
ID and zero replica requests. The binding checks used the real disposable
signer at an expiry 731 ns past a minute boundary. Replaying a signed update
under changed method, arguments, expiry or size failed, as did a mismatched
status ID. The real signer also refused a changed sender and nonzero network
allowance.

signer review below: retain original authority before dispatch, preserve uncertain
outcomes and refuse incompatible reopen. Legacy's retired live backup CLI is not
evidence for this executor.

and data acquisition, allocation/upload/readback/load and stopped completion.
It preserves the populated counter's stable bytes, checks duplicate execute and
lookup, malformed input, wrong caller/arguments/approval/generation, expired new
dispatch and exhausted call-cost allowance. Controller rotation revokes the old
caller's lookup access while the new controller can observe the original record.
Actual management rejection preserves its code/message on replay. Traps before
and after the inner result write leave the original slot Pending across Root upgrade
and duplicate delivery, and blocks subsequent effects. Terminal original replies
remain available after compatible upgrade and expiry. The simulator records a
deletion attempt and clean process cleanup; this is not remote deletion proof.

localhost-enabled execution passed. Early tests exposed Wire's conservative
borrowed-element allocation charge and a cleanup assertion that incorrectly
treated intentional server termination as failure; both are corrected. Root's
snapshot and default layouts remain explicit, without an implicit migration.

## Root records and strict Storage cutover — 2026-11-17

Fresh Root/management now uses registry Storage 0.76.2 `records` and `platform`,
with one participant and two schema-1 roots: journal 16,384 bytes and retained
artifact/source custody 1,048,600 bytes. The supplier admits 17 physical pages
inside the existing 64-page budget. Existing CUJ/CUA payload decoders and authority
checks remain; this is not an importer for 0.74's allocator/Cell image. The earlier
legacy lifecycle/operation research at `377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801`
still applies to the same intent/dispatch/result boundaries; no lifecycle policy
was rederived or replaced in this storage change.

Removed the old MemoryRuntime bootstrap, StableCell adapters and supplier CodecError
coupling. Two concrete record setters persist at the existing mutation sites and
trap on encoding/write failure before replacing their decoded cache. There is no
generic Cell compatibility facade, custom allocator, pre-upgrade flush or reset
fallback. Logical predecessor codecs remain deliberate compatibility evidence.
Root still uses greeting readiness; the counter's managed readiness/maintenance
and interactive calls are the next consumer increment.

Focused installed evidence:

- Generated starter build plus scheduled Root replacement preserves the original
  approval and autonomously creates exactly one child (5.01s).
- Ten affected Root cases pass (29.43s): two-child histories, reverse admission
  order, callback loss and sibling progress, reservations, discovery, metrics,
  CLI maintenance and old-allocator rejection. CLI rejection preserves signed
  installation custody, the old module and bytes, leaving Root stopped; an
  explicit fixture restart verifies retained old custody. Reapply cannot replace
  the failed signed attempt.
- Strict reopen additionally checks an actual reply-encoding trap after journal,
  artifact and timer mutation: both records and scheduled creation roll back.
  Old, empty and corrupt images refuse without initialization (5.76s).

## Installed counter on published Storage — 2026-11-17

`apps/demo` now owns one application crate, its passive Wire request/response
contract and explicit actor lifecycle. Reuse the legacy persistence/upgrade
research at `377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801` under **First Root-owned
child upgrade**: preserve state and authority across failed targets; do not copy
legacy lifecycle engines. The application is deliberately smaller than the
retired profile and needs no Auth, Blob, timers or generalized plugin registry.

Published Storage 0.76.2 owns one 256-byte record reservation, backing and grant.
The real nonanonymous installation caller becomes immutable maintenance authority.
Public increments are checked; explicit monotonic attempts fence writes and only
the matching activation reopens them. Reopen never creates state or activates.
Ordinary domain refusals precede writes; persistence/reply failures trap. Canic's
readiness mount reads actual opened/fenced state. This application-local record
cannot detect authority spent after a restored checkpoint; surviving management
evidence and real Backup integration remain required by Canic #7.

`make counter-test` passes using the actual built Wasms and prepared local server
(4.47 seconds for the final installed case). Two public callers increment; malformed
and forged fields preserve bytes. An additional IC controller cannot fence or
activate, including after that controller performs the B upgrade. A→B→A preserves
value, original authority and closed admission. Repeated readiness cannot activate;
stale activation and reuse of a consumed fence attempt refuse. A target that writes
then traps rolls back both code and data; incompatible schema, corrupt and empty
backing refuse reopen without replacement. One record occupies 65,536 stable bytes.

Executable A is 118,976 bytes, SHA-256
`3034fb15505710f1b3c215e19416ab11ae28da524a42b1d1c91238a7498fc960`;
B is 118,977 bytes, SHA-256
`10b0c7605ab19013ccbdc823463b27539c4042c1bcc9beac85455d1b6077412d`.
Inputs select Storage 0.76.2, Runtime 0.23.17, Wire 0.24.3 and Testkit 0.109.0;
the pre-existing Platform 0.34.15/Timers 0.46.4 selections were preserved.

qualification lock `8250dfcf69b22aad2e59d645097b1255be63b9da7cb02db080b7eb1b2b5bebfa`.
Focused Clippy and Wasm Rustdoc pass. The normal PATH CLI builds/inspects the

## Backup signer integration review — 2026-11-17

Record the maintainer's accepted ownership split in the existing checkpoint
contract: Platform owns exact offline IC envelopes, Tooling the external signer
and bounded process/credential lifecycle, Canic the small Backup adapter, and
Backup the sole host operation/dispatch/recovery owner. Reuse the original-byte,
intent-before-dispatch and uncertainty research at legacy
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801` under **First Root-owned child upgrade**
below. The live legacy backup CLI was unavailable at the design baseline; it is
not an implementation to transplant. The changed assumption is an independently
owned Backup operation journal, not permission to duplicate Canic's dispatcher.

Read the actual Backup `store/operation/runtime::{Signer, SigningRequest}` and
`Connection::sign` plus the public capture/restore facade. Backup wraps the
signer future in a timeout, reserves its allowances first and rechecks admission
after completion. This makes future-drop cleanup a concrete Tooling obligation:
dropping a blocking worker's observer alone cannot terminate or reap the signer.
The [supplier finding](https://github.com/dragginzgame/ic-tooling/issues/12#issuecomment-6957965469)
records the exact caller and needed cancellation handoff without prescribing a
new worker implementation in Canic.

[Platform #29](https://github.com/dragginzgame/ic-platform/issues/29) now records
local offline signing qualification for both Ed25519 and secp256k1, including the
single original-request path and exact expiry. Publication and Canic selection
remain separate. Tooling has no qualified public signer interface to consume yet.
No speculative adapter, supplier patch, key export, dependency or backup command
was added. This is contract/source review only, not an installed Backup pass;
the independent direct-controller witness can proceed once that interface exists
without waiting for Canic's Storage 0.76 runtime.

Follow-up registry/source review on 2026-11-17: Platform 0.34.14 is selected in
both locks, but `ic-storage@0.76.2` and `ic-host-sign` are not published. Tooling's
pending signer retains cancellation custody and explicitly refuses independent
status admission. Its existing [Platform finding](https://github.com/dragginzgame/ic-platform/issues/29#issuecomment-6958534088)
identifies the missing constructor for authenticating returned status bytes from
the original request ID and approved sender, without an original update envelope.
This matches Backup's actual `Signing::Observation` input. No duplicate issue,
local crypto/admission workaround or supplier source change was needed. A search
of the active product/build paths found no remaining `apps/profile` references;
the retained profile runners select the qualification manifest. This follow-up
is source and availability review, not additional installed qualification.

future-time window; restoring the existing policy passed the installed workflow.

the existing public synthetic key. Remove the dual Platform alias, redundant
principal conversions and old normal/dev dependency. New rejection diagnostics
store the provider's numeric code as a string; old stored rejections remain readable.

## Early CI setup diagnostics — 2026-11-17

The maintainer selected Canic only after concurrent DB/Query repairs appeared.
No sibling source is changed. Policy-tool, compiler/common-tool and dependency
preparation now run through a small Perl wrapper available before tool setup.
The child uses a job-owned temporary tool store; existing download caching is
unchanged. Retain a sanitized 64 KiB command tail and at most four explicitly
reported `install.log` excerpts, each limited to 16 KiB source/output plus a
truncation marker. Attempt paths may include hidden directories but must remain
inside that store, with no descendant symlinks or nonregular files. Nothing scans
or uploads an entire tool store, home, environment or payload directory.

Known secret environment values, sensitive assignments/fields, credential URLs,
URL parameters, bearer/GitHub tokens and private-key blocks are omitted/redacted
before console/artifact publication. Oversized diagnostic lines are omitted whole.
The wrapper is scoped to the selected public setup commands, not arbitrary
application payload logging. Original exit/signal status survives collection
failures; collection errors are reported separately. Failure-only uploads have
seven-day retention and unique job/host/attempt names. The job summary links the
artifact when available and explicitly reports missing/upload-failed evidence.

`bash scripts/ci/test-setup-diagnostics.sh` passes early failure with a hidden
attempt log, redaction, bounded output, missing evidence, symlink refusal,
collector failure, success, signals and pipeline failure. It also exercises the
workflow's actual upload-result reporting shell for success and failure.
Actionlint, ShellCheck and Perl syntax checks pass. These are offline Linux
fixtures under `target/ci-setup-tests/`, not observed hosted artifact retrieval,
macOS execution or a product/release gate. The existing broader collector was
not selected: it archives tool candidates and does not sanitize their contents.
No new package, product dependency or runtime mechanism is introduced.

  fails because Timers requires Runtime `=0.22.5`; both exact runtime guards

patch the singleton guard or introduce a second executor, copied supplier,
unused new dependency or direct external IC imports. The design now records this
updated boundary instead of claiming Runtime's Auth APIs are unimplemented.

## Optional issuer mounts and local-key installed witness: 2026-11-16

and three explicit mounts. It reuses the existing Auth endpoint hooks for
pre-copy guards, directional budgets and trap-on-encoding-failure behavior.
It starts no engine or lifecycle and adds no product crate or default dependency.
The qualification application explicitly selects `MemoryIssuer`; account policy
and the local public test key stay with that application. Its permitted account
differs from the installation controller. Auth owns the sole login/intent/result
and renewal-sequence state, including work/result reservations and real pruning.

Root-enrolled receiver verification, browser-bound renewal with unchanged login
deadline, lookup after permission loss/expiry, malformed framing, physical
capacity, real pruning, callback encoding rollback, permission changes during
entropy, expired entropy completion and closed upgrade loss. A local callback
trap rolls signature/result/counter writes back together while the earlier
reservation remains unresolved. No remote signer effect is present. Focused
native/Wasm Clippy and Rustdoc pass. Host's normal graph still imports only passive
Auth types/Wire; default `canic` imports neither supplier engine nor Auth contracts.

## Direct Root Auth enrollment: 2026-11-16

The existing optional adapter now has an explicit `auth-enrollment` capability;
Root's optional `auth` feature uses its existing controller guard to approve one
immutable receiver/audience/issuer/key/role/scope grant. The receiver uses real IC
randomness and an opaque consuming direct-call ticket. No relayed snapshot or
claimed response principal can become authenticated enrollment. Complete bounded
content and canonical commitments are checked before the real Auth engine's
metadata and Canic's entry projection publish in the same callback. No ordinary
fallible work follows metadata publication. A trapped callback retains its
original pending window; expiry permits a fresh attempt that fences late results.

receiver, audience, challenge, deadline, canonical entries and late replies.

## Host removal and explicit restoration: 2026-11-16

The existing plan/journal/signed-request owners now cover removal and restoration.
Removal is one v4 PlanAction with exact source publication, Root/Store/code and
content bindings. Its successful receipt is derived from the original signed
request and matching reply, not a separately trusted summary. Reconcile refuses
to prepare a first signature. Every known matching publication mutation must
have settled and expired before removal; a later observation cannot erase an
ambiguous original call. Publication guards cover independent and nested fleet
execution under the same journal lock.

Restoration reuses publication with an optional exact removal predecessor in its
approval digest. Every known removal must be an ancestor with a matching successful
reply and expired dispatch window; ordinary old receipts cannot revive authority.
Historical v1/v2/v3 bytes remain unchanged when the new fields are absent. There
is no new Root journal, allocation, catalog, retry owner or package. The CLI's
artifact selection/dispatch was consolidated into its existing artifact module.
Legacy research recorded under the versioned Store decision still applies:
immutable labels/content and explicit custody are retained; mutable replacement
and copied Root catalogs are not brought back.

The actual CLI/ICP/PocketIC witness
`bash scripts/dev/test-publication.sh cli_removal_restoration` passes (44.49s):
publication quiescence, shared bytes under distinct labels, exact deletion,
one retained signature across apply/reconcile, refused early restoration,
new-authority restoration, expired historical payload replay, and a remotely
committed cancellation whose lost Root reply remains blocked despite absence.
Root stable bytes remain unchanged. The existing two-child whole-fleet bootstrap,
publication and serial-upgrade CLI regression also passes (145.08s), exercising
the shared journal argument on nested paths. Native checks pass: 137 Host tests (one
separate preparation-dependent test ignored) and 70 CLI tests, including historical
approval digests and three removal/history regressions. Focused Host/CLI and
installed-harness Clippy, Host Rustdoc, formatting and tooling agreement pass.
`make canic` installs the commands on PATH; no browser is launched.

below. Tested Store Wasm is
`2d6bb886db5049a2e06f292d13e462c38b0a95463a560fcb71dab5f52efa567b`;
fault-enabled Root is
`99ef76ade50088f158c15eb9964da11def7535174282fb5753de2bdf970e3aee`.

The selected path starts from independent publication approvals and keeps reviewed

## Root exact removal boundary: 2026-11-16

delete or cancellation. Store retains pin, identity, tombstone and quota ownership.

are reused; the wait policy is explicit at each call site. Removal uses unbounded
wait, while existing metadata/admission calls retain their five-second bounded
wait. No Root catalog, retry journal or new allocation was introduced.

`bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh removal` passes its maximum-manifest frame check
and two installed cases (6.96s): wrong identity/action, pinned content, malformed
and unauthorized ingress, maintenance fencing, shared-byte retention, tombstone
quota, cancellation/restoration, and expired historical requests after restoration.
The fixture waits for the exact successful result and expires the original
dispatch deadline before restoring. An injected response-encoding trap demonstrates

## Persistent profile growth after infrastructure maintenance: 2026-11-16

Pending 0.217.0 now qualifies the second-child path with the selected persistent
consumer, extending `profile_store` rather than adding another bootstrap fixture.

records stay untouched. Each worker owns its namespace and permissions.

retains both creation records, the original worker's two upgrades and the added
worker's upgrade. Root, Store and both workers preserve every stable byte across
that replacement. Both users can read their own complete profiles; cross-user and
Root reads remain Forbidden. Fleet inspection reports all four canisters without
inventory issues. The existing interrupted-install/reconciliation and incompatible
application readiness cases still run in the same witness.

## Repeat Store maintenance under the existing owner: 2026-11-15

The pending 0.216.0 increment extends the existing Store maintenance journal and
fleet planner. Root retains up to eight immutable attempts, archives only Ready
predecessors and admits the next sequence against the exact Store/code boundary.
Every callback mutation matches both approval and phase; historical replay returns
its own evidence without touching a successor. Full history is never evicted.
The original 16 KiB Storage lease remains unchanged. The worst-field codec fixture
round-trips two full child journals plus eight Store attempts in 12,880 payload
bytes. CUJ7 explicitly migrates recognized predecessors, including CUJ6; older
executables refuse the new tag. No Wasm payload enters Root's journal.

New Host approvals bind the sequence and select an exact-attempt read. Historical
plan bytes, digests, deadlines and signed read methods remain unchanged. Root
maintenance after a second attempt binds every predecessor and selects the new
complete-history read; the older read refuses to omit history. Child delivery
selects that same contract, and publication reuse follows the validated Store
code chain. No CLI command, crate, dependency, allocator, workflow engine or
supplier change was added. The supplier remains at
`541d911410dae0f38eefc371693024bf2068c55b` and owns artifact bytes and admission.

Reused the recorded legacy immutable-intent, late-callback and serial-upgrade
research at `377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801`, especially the
[bounded serial child upgrade decision](#bounded-serial-child-upgrades) and
[first Store workflow](#fleet-commands-carry-store-replacement-through-later-upgrades-2026-11-15).
The applicable lesson is preserving original authority through continuations,
not importing the legacy workflow catalogue. The supplier admission implementation
already supports successive exact attempts; Canic required the missing orchestration
and custody boundary, not another Store mechanism.

Focused Linux qualification passed:

- 108 Host tests and nine protocol tests; four affected maintenance tests were
  rerun after adding full-history omission/substitution cases. The installed Host
  entrypoint ignored in native tests ran through the Store harness.
- All 25 installed Store cases. New witnesses migrate both a completed operation
  and an issued installation from the retained 0.215 Root, complete eight Store
  upgrades, recover the second upload, preserve all prior records and stable Store
  bytes, refuse stale/reused/mismatched authority and a ninth attempt, and reject
  older Root code without losing custody. A broad regression exposed changed
  Occupied/Time precedence; the original occupied-authority precedence was restored.
- The installed CLI/signer journey (80.38s) completes two Store replacements with
  interrupted second-upload recovery, preserves both through Root maintenance,

- Both manager growth/upgrade cases passed; the optional external historical

The installed callback-recovery work exposed misleading native CLI guidance:
UpgradePending always printed another apply command, even for a retained uncertain
installation needing explicit reconciliation. Repeating that command can consume
the bounded observation slots without requesting recovery. Share the existing
status projection's upgrade decision with progress output: issued upload/install
and supported failures select reconcile, ordinary progress/Ready-without-receipt
select apply, and Empty/Expired/ReserveBlocked select status for review. Retain
the original operation, scope and signer via the existing shell-safe renderer.
No new command, alias, Host authority, JSON field or management behavior is added.
Store and whole-fleet creation continuations retain their existing owners.

Reused the recorded legacy diagnostics/custody research at
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801`: presentation must preserve uncertainty,
scope and original effect authority. Status and progress now consume one upgrade
guidance function instead of contradictory decisions. The touched progress tests
move to their adjacent test module. Focused cases cover all upgrade phases,
failures, shell quoting, retained scope/signers, and unchanged outcome evidence.

All 15 status and three progress tests pass, together with CLI Clippy/Rustdoc,
formatting and diff checks. `make canic` installs the development CLI through the
normal PATH. A read-only `canic fleet status` against the earlier callback-failure
fixture reports InstallIssued, directs original-operation recovery and retains
the absence of a receipt. Evidence: `target/qualification/upgrade-guidance-*.log`.
No simulator rerun was needed for this presentation-only change. Package metadata
still reports 0.212.0; the pending increment remains 0.213.0, local and unpushed.

## Profile worker callback recovery: 2026-11-15

Continued pending 0.213.0 at the remaining installed composition boundary: a worker
upgrade can commit remotely while its callback traps before Root retains success.
Reused the existing management engine's fault point 8, the earlier installed
growth upgrade cases, and the legacy custody/readiness research below. No new
fault endpoint, retry policy or recovery engine was needed.

This exposed a product defect: the timer set its per-slot heap exclusion before
awaiting an effect, but a trapping callback never reached the manual clear. The
existing growth fixtures replaced the owner before reconciliation, hiding the
stuck flag. Replace manual acquire/clear pairs in scheduling and both reconciliation
paths with one task-owned standard-library DropGuard. Runtime 0.22.5's call cleanup
cancels and drops the suspended task; dropping the guard clears only heap exclusion,
never durable intent, reservations or attempt counts. No timeout, forced unlock,
new lease record or supplier change is involved.

Additional read-only legacy inspection at
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801` covered
`crates/canic-core/src/workflow/topology/guard.rs` and its path history
(`ed9af4e1b35cad3a0c5622521dd64abe02d5fb4b`,
`538c87924e8f719a471ece8ffa7558785922a328`,
`173fb90f7fc41c3c2afa1f0425780e28d2ad8c0b`), plus
`crates/canic-core/src/ops/storage/async_job_recovery/tests.rs` and the
0.104 B3 domain async-job recovery report. Preserve scoped destruction and the
tests' separation between operation identity and execution exclusion. Do not port
legacy lease takeover or watchdog machinery: this path has an executor-owned
cleanup boundary and cannot reissue uncertain management effects.

The profile witness bootstraps normally, then selects the existing fault-enabled
manager through an ordinary maintenance approval. After the worker upgrade,
the new executable serves the unchanged private records while Root remains at
InstallIssued. Replanning returns the same operation; another apply observes
uncertainty without a completion receipt. Explicit `fleet reconcile` reconstructs
the approved outcome. The test checks retained approval, exact signed admission,
effect fingerprints and counts, and precisely one child version increment. It
then continues through Store maintenance, replacement with the normal manager,
private writes and a subsequent worker upgrade. All fault selection is local,
with synthetic cycles and the fixture's isolated identity.

The first run caught a qualification reader using profile limits for management
status. It now uses the management protocol's declared limits for both encoding
and decoding; product limits and admission checks are unchanged. This proves a
recoverable committed upgrade, not arbitrary rejected or unknown installations.

The installed profile CLI witness passes in 61.49s. The affected inline growth
suite passes both current tests in 14.73s (one optional predecessor fixture remains
ignored), including creation upload/install callback recovery before owner
maintenance. The two-child lost-creation/sibling-upgrade witness passes in 7.26s.
Runtime Wasm Clippy/Rustdoc with all features, focused Root/growth qualification
Clippy, generated-app lint, formatting, shell syntax and diff checks pass.
Evidence: `target/qualification/profile-store-recovery-*.log`. No mainnet effects
or delivery occurred. The fix requires the updated management executable; recovery
of a guard already stranded in an older deployed Root is not established here.

## Ready-to-dispatch Host and CLI journey: 2026-11-15

Connected the qualified runtime contract to fleet and named child planning.
New plans use one captured timestamp for plan creation and earliest dispatch,
with expiry ten minutes later. Host's single admission-cutoff function uses that
expiry only when the two timestamps are equal; explicit future timestamps keep
their earlier pre-dispatch cutoff. All historically valid plans had strictly
future dispatch, so their original digests, cutoffs and signed attempts remain
unchanged. Replanning still returns the retained approval, including after expiry.
Bootstrap and artifact publication consume the same finite window; no phase gets
an independent renewal. Existing Root code may refuse late admission, so it needs
an explicit future schedule or separately reviewed maintenance before this default.

Reused the legacy/bootstrap analysis and runtime proof in the following entry.
The change adds no wire field, serialized timing mode, endpoint, configuration
switch, scheduler or retry owner. Plan/expiry validation admits equality but still
refuses dispatch before plan creation. The existing CLI help and status renderer
now describe readiness instead of showing a fictitious future wait; generated
starter instructions and current demo/design guidance use the same workflow.

Twenty focused Host tests passed, including historical digest fixtures, repeated
planning, named defaults, explicit cutoffs, and expired unsubmitted immediate and
scheduled fleets refusing to sign creation. Seventeen CLI status/progress cases,
Host/CLI and publication-harness Clippy, Host Rustdoc, formatting and diff checks
passed. The installed publication Store-upgrade journey passed in 49.38 seconds:
default fleet plan/apply created Root, Store and app in 7,873 ms, then after Store
recovery and Root maintenance a default child plan/apply upgraded the existing app
in 4,870 ms. The latter timing excludes its earlier artifact publication. Neither
uses `--execute-at` or simulated clock jumps. These are individual local samples,
not mainnet performance measurements or comparisons with the earlier publication-only
baseline. Existing lost-reply, original-authority and complete-custody checks remain.

No push, mainnet operation or browser launch was performed.

## Earliest-dispatch runtime admission: 2026-11-15

The latency investigation found two meanings attached to `execute_at_ns`: the
earliest timer dispatch and the last acceptable admission time. The protocol now
uses it only as the earliest dispatch bound. Creation and upgrade admission accept
that timestamp at or before current time while the original expiry is still future.
Creation keeps its ten-minute interval bound. Upgrade explicitly bounds the
original interval to one hour, preserving the restriction previously implied by
future-only admission plus the one-hour expiry horizon. No timestamp, deadline,
request commitment or retry allowance is rewritten.

Reused the pinned legacy Root-bootstrap and retained-work research under
[the Root extraction](#one-child-root-implementation)
and the [Store decision](#versioned-wasm-store-decision-2026-11-15): revision
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801`, Root bootstrap
`crates/canic-control-plane/src/workflow/bootstrap/root/mod.rs`, publication chunk
custody and bootstrap diagnostic history `ed9f133`. Their invariant is retained
authority/artifact custody before deferred work and truthful readiness. They do
not require a fixed ten-minute delay. The current Root already has a durable
approval and one timer owner; changing admission needs neither another scheduler
nor a new endpoint, wire shape, stable record or supplier.

`bash scripts/dev/test-store.sh dispatch` passed two installed witnesses in 8.05s.
One uses the real Root and Store to create and upgrade after the earliest timestamp,
without an operator continuation. Exact approvals, attempts, references and installed
modules survive Root maintenance; expired admission leaves state unchanged and exact
replays after expiry retain completed evidence. The other proves future work still
waits and a timer first run after expiry performs no creation. Native boundary
checks cover equality, expiry, oversized intervals and integer extremes. Protocol
and installed-harness Clippy, runtime Wasm Clippy, protocol Rustdoc, formatting,
shell syntax and diff checks passed. Logs: `target/qualification/ready-dispatch-*`.

This qualifies the runtime step, not an immediate-dispatch Host journey. The Host
still defaults to ten minutes and refuses fresh admission after that time. The next
step must bind readiness to the immutable plan and verify the public CLI path before
changing that default; older Roots remain unsupported for elapsed-time admission.
No mainnet effects or release were performed. The maintainer confirmed preserving
the pre-existing `rand 0.10.4` lockfile update; its input hashes stayed unchanged
during these checks. Earlier local 0.211.1 status and measurement work is retained.

## Local publication measurement baseline: 2026-11-15

Extended the existing installed publication witness rather than adding a benchmark
runner, CLI mode or runtime metrics owner. This follows design section 12 and the
[versioned Store research](#versioned-wasm-store-decision-2026-11-15): legacy

latency benchmark. No scheduling or authority contract changed.

The first measurement assertion incorrectly equated file size with uploaded
payload. The real installed run exposed existing intra-Wasm chunk reuse. The
corrected witness independently hashes input chunks, compares unique counts/bytes
with decoded retained Put requests, and requires zero requests for warm reuse.
The padded 2,044,909-byte artifact retained three unique Put payloads totaling
734,189 bytes; its second version retained zero. A passing local sample measured:

| Command segment | Host wall time |
| --- | ---: |
| Cold plan | 54 ms |
| Prepare interrupted before first chunk signing | 1,131 ms |
| Observation-only reconcile | 747 ms |
| Resume upload and publish | 2,292 ms |
| Warm version plan | 59 ms |
| Warm version publication | 1,343 ms |

These are command durations including signer/transport/observation overhead, not
isolated Store execution or full deployment latency. Builds, replica setup, Store
bootstrap, child scheduling/installations and cycles are excluded. Payload totals
describe retained completed requests, not network retransmissions. No speedup,
mainnet performance or timing threshold is claimed; original ten-minute default
dispatch timestamps remain unchanged.

The selected installed journey passed in 29.57 seconds, including its existing

## Root installs and activates its Store: 2026-11-15

Continued pending 0.210.0 from the bounded preservation scan. Reused the legacy
manifest/custody and checkpoint interruption research recorded below; legacy did
not establish the rewrite's upgrade guarantee. Inspected the existing child
upgrade owner and Platform's actual Wasm management exports. `InstallCodeArgs`
is native-only in Platform 0.33.7, so the runtime uses its existing
`UploadChunkArgs`/`InstallChunkedCodeArgs` contract instead of adding a parallel
management type or supplier change. The
[management specification](https://docs.internetcomputer.org/references/ic-interface-spec/management-canister/#ic-method-install_chunked_code)
defines installation from the controlled same-subnet management cache.

Explicit installation supplies at most 1 MiB of approved raw Wasm. Root uploads
once into Store's management cache and retains the upload commitment, then issues
one exact chunked-install request with its original sender version. Host owns the
durable bytes; Root never stores them. Installation uncertainty is resolved only
by approved module/sole-controller/stopped observation. The continuation starts
Store with admission closed and repeats the same metadata scan. Only a complete
matching result admits explicit activation of the original attempt. No implicit
activation, deadline renewal or upload/install/start/activation replay exists.
Completion releases mutation fences but preserves the operation; the old child-only
custody surface still refuses it, including Ready, so Host cannot omit that record.

The existing unreleased CUJ6 shape now includes installation and before/after
preservation evidence. CUJ5 and earlier released shapes retain explicit migration;
no compatibility lane was added for an intermediate, unshipped CUJ6 shape. The
maximum maintenance receipt is 910 encoded bytes in the envelope probe. Its
scoped budget preserves the 4 KiB frame bound while allowing 256 KiB traversal
allocation, 2,048 nodes and depth 16; child observation budgets are unchanged.
The first budget probe exposed the original allocation limit and was corrected
before installed qualification. An initial installed assertion also used an
invalid publication deadline; its rejection was correct and the fixture now
uses publication's own shorter window. Failed attempts remain recorded in
`store-install-budget-probe.log` and `store-install-installed.log`.

Four installed maintenance cases pass (7.71s), covering callback faults 15–18,
pre-install dispatch rollback at fault 19, Root replacement with uncertain effect
evidence, wrong bytes/callers/malformed requests, expired dispatch, rejected
modules and a replacement lacking the required admission endpoint. The successful
path compares every Store stable byte before/after upgrade, including all payloads,
shared-version pins and pending upload state, then resumes publication and child
admission after explicit activation. A separate expiry case recovers installed
code but cannot issue a new start. Runtime verification itself compares metadata,
not all payload hashes. No general corruption-repair guarantee is claimed.
The focused completion rerun (7.79s) also refuses child-only custody after Ready
and proves that replaying the completed activation cannot reopen a newer Store
gate. The maximum 1 MiB installation frame round-trips through its scoped budget
and remains refused by the unchanged smaller worker-staging budget.

The four existing quiescence/historical-migration cases pass (7.24s), as do the
Wire-budget test, native/Wasm/fixture Clippy, Rustdoc and formatting. Required
`make growth-test` passes both installed cases (14.44s); its optional historical
manager input remains ignored. Normal manager footprint is 1,070,794 raw bytes,
763,202 code bytes and 18 stable pages. Shared journal decoding adds measured
binary cost even when Store-maintenance mounts are absent; no additional storage
allocation, dependency, crate, timer, catalog mirror or generic workflow engine
was added. Logs use `target/qualification/store-install-*.log`.

Remaining limits: one retained maintenance attempt, no reset/cancellation or
successor history, no automatic repair after rejection/expiry, no larger-than-one-
chunk infrastructure delivery, and no Host/CLI/mainnet qualification. The current
Host Store code bindings cannot advance until that operation is integrated into
existing plan/apply/status custody. Package versions and installed CLI remain
0.209.0. This increment is local, with no commit, push or live deployment.

## Closed Store catalog preservation: 2026-11-15

Started pending 0.210.0 after reviewing the 0.209.0 push. Reused the legacy
immutable manifest/chunk and snapshot/reopen research at
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801`, plus the checkpoint sequence/late-callback
research recorded below. The changed requirement is an external, bounded
preservation commitment under the rewrite's exact Store closure. Legacy's mutable
catalog replacement and checkpoint orchestrator remain excluded.

Store now returns one ordered release-custody entry under its exact closed
attempt and captured controller. Each page includes invariant quota/counters;
the entry includes aliases, incomplete-upload progress and every shared pin.
Root advances one page per call, retaining only a digest, cursor and count in its
existing journal. Concurrent responses can commit the same cursor only once.
Strict key progression, unchanged counters, the complete release count and an
explicit end page precede stop. There is no payload scan, catalog copy, new
dependency/allocation/timer, installation path or CLI command.

A native migration probe rejected the initial assumption that Serde's default
field could decode the prior Wire record (`Malformed`). Removed that assumption
and added an explicit CUJ5 decoder before writing CUJ6. The retained normal Root
from the 0.209.0 gate is now an installed fixture, with provenance and checksum in
`qualification/tests/fixtures/README.md`. An initial fixture setup attempted an
unsupported downgrade from CUJ6 and correctly trapped; corrected it to install
the predecessor fresh. Real Closed and Stopped CUJ5 attempts now preserve every
authority/stop field, with absent preservation evidence remaining absent. Closed
attempts may scan; already-stopped attempts cannot gain invented evidence.

Focused validation passes: ten Store native cases, both protocol suites and
maximum metadata/status envelopes; four installed quiescence cases (7.19s),
including Root replacement mid-scan and concurrent continuations; and the required
shared-engine growth regression (two passing installed cases, 14.67s; optional
older-manager input ignored). Focused native/Wasm/fixture Clippy, Rustdoc and
formatting pass. Logs use `target/qualification/store-preservation-*.log`.
The growth witness measures the normal manager at 1,045,965 raw bytes, 746,707 code
bytes and 18 stable pages; the shared journal codec has a measurable binary cost
even though that manager does not mount Store-maintenance endpoints.

The full upgrade remains blocked: complete payload preservation, installation,
restart and matching activation still need qualification. Stores lacking the new
page contract cannot complete this scan; no fallback grants replacement authority.
Package versions and installed CLI remain 0.209.0; 0.210.0 is a local pending
increment, with no additional push or mainnet effect.

retains intent before enqueueing, with separate callback faults 13/14. Recovery

boundary; no exact canister-version increment is assumed.

## Store admission survives maintenance: 2026-11-15

Continued the pending 0.209.0 increment with Store-local mutation admission.
Additional read-only legacy research at
`377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801` covered
`workflow/application_checkpoint/mod.rs`, `ops/application_checkpoint/mod.rs`
and `ops/application_checkpoint/tests.rs` under `crates/canic-control-plane/src`.
The test `original_unit_storage_replay_and_release_fence_delayed_prepares`
demonstrates exact operation/sequence selection, persistence, idempotent replay,
mutation closure and rejection of delayed acknowledgements. The workflow also
rechecks its authority after await. Reviewed the file history at `d704f4d` and
`aed32be`. Adapted these invariants; did not import participant discovery,
installation registries, checkpoint orchestration or the older Store retirement
workflow (`workflow/wasm_store/mod.rs`), which solves a different lifecycle.

Store persists the latest sequence, operation, captured controller and closed
phase in CWS2 settings under its original allocation. Only the next sequence may
close an open gate; only the exact attempt/owner can activate it. Replaying Close
after activation observes that completed phase rather than closing again.
Unknown/corrupt records fail; recognized CWS1 settings migrate without inventing
a prior attempt. Quota changes preserve the gate. This is a one-way payload codec
transition, not support for downgrading the executable.

All publication/pin/quota mutations check the gate; reads remain available.
Cache work captures an epoch before dispatch and rechecks it after awaits and
before returning readiness. A close/reopen cycle fences old continuations even
when the current phase is open. Already-issued cache uploads can finish; closure
is not a drain certificate. Root's fixed `store_admission` mount reuses current
controller/deadline admission and the bounded metadata transport. Store commits
survive a Root reply trap and are recovered through exact observation/replay.

Focused Linux validation: nine Store native tests and the metadata budget test
pass. Two installed admission cases pass (7.13s): closed admission survives Store
and Root replacement, pins/incomplete uploads persist, wrong-owner and stale
activation fail, and an actual in-flight cache repair is fenced while a fresh
repair after activation succeeds. The existing cold/warm/cache-loss installation
and child upgrade witness also passes (6.60s), using hash-verified unchanged
greeting artifacts. Focused native/Wasm and fixture Clippy, Wasm Rustdoc, formatting
and shell syntax pass. A redundant clone in a test was removed during linting.
Logs are `target/qualification/store-admission-{native,installed,cache-regression,lint,doc}.log`.

The installed fixture deliberately retains direct operator control to upgrade
Store; it does not impersonate Root or qualify the Root-only installation path.
CWS1 migration is covered by its actual record codec, not a historical installed

## Whole-fleet Store bootstrap: 2026-11-15

The existing fleet envelope now admits one declared Store alongside Root and one
or two application children. Reuse the legacy bootstrap-ordering research below
and the qualified named Store action: infrastructure readiness precedes dependent
application admission, while original output references and effect custody stay
with their existing owners. No legacy scheduler, catalog mirror or implicit
service activation was imported.

Store binds Root's approved creation output, its own exact executable and a
separate 1T operator debit. Whole-fleet `--cycles` is the total, divided into that
Store debit and Root funding (which already covers child grants). Defaults are
4T for one app plus Store and 5T for two. Store's nested journal uses the existing
direct creation/install/handover/readiness path. Both application slots remain
available. A lost handover reply is recoverable before child admission;
reconcile never admits those children, and apply retains their original schedule.
Cancellation, inventory, status and convergence all include Store. Existing
one/two-child approval fixtures retain their exact digests and output layouts
when Store is absent.

Nine focused fleet tests and eight CLI status tests pass. The installed
`make publication-test` runner passes both the named publication journey and a
four-canister fleet witness (18.67s together): pause after Store handover, prove
neither child was admitted, recover the removed local handover reply, then admit
and complete both children. Root's stable state remains unchanged until application
admission. Replay creates nothing extra, four-canister inventory has no issues,
no-change uses retained completion, and missing Store readiness proof refuses
convergence. The first native run caught a changed no-Store reconciliation error;
restoring the original admission ordering resolved it. Host/CLI and installed
harness Clippy, Rustdoc and the retained-digest regressions pass. Logs:
`target/qualification/fleet-store-*.log`.

The simplification is composition over the existing owners, with one explicit
infrastructure field rather than another application slot or lifecycle engine.
Store remains optional and the generated starter remains minimal. Store artifact
replacement and addition/removal on retained fleets are deliberately refused;
adoption and maintenance need their own qualification. Application deployment
still uses inline payloads; selecting a published Store release is the next
integration gap. No mainnet action or push accompanies this work.

## Fleet CLI artifact publication: 2026-11-15

The existing Host plan/apply/status/reconcile owners now publish one explicit
release into an already-bound Root-owned Store. V3 approval binds exact artifact
bytes, label, network, operator and both canister/code identities. The journal
lock, signer admission, retained attempts and immutable file owner are reused.
V1/v2 deployment digest fixtures remain unchanged. Publication does not reserve
an application slot or appear as an installed canister in fleet inventory.

The [preceding publication research](#publication-through-root-after-store-handover-2026-11-15)
at legacy revision `377fbceb3994c9d1f3033979ef4e4ce9e6b85801` covers this same
question: preserve exact release identity, bounded transfer and observable
progress, without the legacy Root catalog/cache workflow. The current qualified
Host signer already owns durable request custody, so no legacy transport or
second journal/executor was added. Store remains the only durable catalog/byte
owner; Root performs one typed call at a time.

Every mutation retains its original dispatch deadline and signature. Fresh
recovery authority can only observe that exact release, including after effect
approval expiry. Exact observation can establish a lost mutation's outcome,
but cannot authorize remaining expired work. A completion summary alone is
insufficient: its retained observation index, signed request, response and exact
manifest must agree. Thirty-two observations bound this initial recovery history;
exhaustion blocks without discarding evidence.

Focused validation passed: seventeen native cases across publication admission,
retained operation encoding, signer custody and CLI argument conflicts; the
installed `make publication-test` witness passed in 12.79s. The witness uses the
actual CLI, pinned ICP signer, Root and Store with synthetic cycles. It interrupts
before the first chunk signature, reconciles without mutation, resumes without
replacing Prepare's signature, publishes two versions with warm byte reuse and
proves unchanged Root stable memory. It also refuses wrong certified code and
missing signed receipt proof, checks human status, and keeps publications out of
canister inventory. Host/CLI and the new harness passed focused Clippy; Host
Rustdoc passed. Logs are `target/qualification/publication-{host-tests,host-clippy,host-doc,installed}.log`.

Review removed misleading deployment-receipt text from publication status and
kept inventory sampling under one lock. It also found that the old greeting-child
bootstrap could admit a declared Store as an application. Whole-fleet and named
child planning now refuse that unsupported role before journal creation; the
focused regression proves both refusals. A separate Store bootstrap/readiness
contract and deployment release selection are the next coherent integration,
not another workflow engine. Current publication requires existing bound owners;
Store maintenance through Root and existing-fleet migration remain pending.
Artifact publication admits 8 MiB; the current public builder/inspector still
admits 1.5 MB, so larger inputs need an external build. No mainnet deployment,
measured deployment speedup, browser launch, version bump or push is claimed.
